===== Page 17 =====

Doubly Linked List
Singly vs. Doubly Linked Lists
   
© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026 
2 / 9
B.TECH » DEPARTMENT OF COMPUTER SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12
Worst-Case Operation (n nodes)
Singly Linked List
Doubly Linked List
Move Forward
1 step
1 step
Move Backward
n steps (Requires re-traversal)
1 step (Instant fallback)
Insert Before a Node
n steps (Find predecessor)
1 step (Using prev_ link)
Delete a Designated Node
n steps (Find predecessor)
1 step (Local isolation)
Searching for a Key
n steps
n steps

© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026 
2 / 9
B.TECH » DEPARTMENT OF COMPUTER SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12
1. Bidirectional traversal
2. Easy deletion
3. Easy insertion before or after a known node
4. Natural representation of navigation

© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026

2. More pointer manipulation
3. More opportunities for bugs
4. Still has linear search

•
Initialize / Deinitialize the queue
•

•

•
Modify rear

•

•

•
Modify front

Queue

© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026

B.TECH » DEPARTMENT OF COMPUTER SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12
•
CPU scheduling : processes in batch processing usually wait in a queue
•
Printer Spooler : collects the jobs for printing in a queue
•
Breadth First search : Looking for a key in a tree structure 
•
Simulation models : queues to simulate random events in queueing theory
•
Message queues : sharing information in operating systems
•
Toll booth : waiting of vehicles to pass through a toll booth

#define MAXSIZE 5
struct queue 
{
int key_[MAXSIZE];
int front_;
int rear_;
};
typedef struct queue queue_t;

Queue
Queue: implementation
   
© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026 
2 / 9
B.TECH » DEPARTMENT OF COMPUTER SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12
•
Initialize / Deinitialize the queue
•
Initialize : make head and tail 0
•
Deinitialize : make head and tail 0
void init_queue(queue_t *ptr_queue)
{
// could be any valid index in the array
ptr_queue->rear_ = ptr_queue->front_ = 0; 
}
void deinit_queue(queue_t *ptr_queue) // NO LOOP!
{
ptr_queue->rear_ = ptr_queue->front_ = 0;
}

----------------------------------------

===== Page 15 =====

Queue
Queue: implementation
   
© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026 
2 / 9
B.TECH » DEPARTMENT OF COMPUTER SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12
// to be called before deque before incrementing
int is_empty(queue_t *ptr_queue)
{
return ptr_queue->front_ == ptr_queue->rear_;
}
// to be called in enque after incrementing
int is_full(queue_t *ptr_queue)
{
return ptr_queue->front_ == ptr_queue->rear_;
}
•
is_full and is_empty : same code!!

----------------------------------------

===== Page 16 =====

Queue
Queue: implementation : enque and deque
   
© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026 
2 / 9
B.TECH » DEPARTMENT OF COMPUTER SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12
void enque(queue_t *ptr_queue, int key)
{
int temp;
temp = ++ptr_queue->rear_ % MAXSIZE;
ptr_queue->rear_ = temp;
if(! is_full(ptr_queue))  {
ptr_queue->key_[temp] = key;
}
else {
ptr_queue->rear_ = (ptr_queue->rear_ - 1) % MAXSIZE;
printf("queue full; cannot push\n");
}
}

----------------------------------------

===== Page 17 =====

Queue
Queue: implementation : enque and deque
   
© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026 
2 / 9
B.TECH » DEPARTMENT OF COMPUTER SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12
int deque(queue_t *ptr_queue)
{
if(! is_empty(ptr_queue)) {
int key;  int temp = ++ptr_queue->front_ % MAXSIZE;
key = ptr_queue->key_[temp];
ptr_queue->front_ = temp;
return key;
} else {
printf("queue empty; cannot pop\n");
return '\0';
}
}

•

•

•

•
Toll booth : waiting of vehicles to pass through a toll booth

© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026

•
A priority queue is an abstract data type in which each element is associated 
with a priority.
•
A priority queue removes the component with the highest priority first.
•
A priority is a number associated with each element
•
Higher the numeric value, could mean higher the priority. 
•
Or lower the numeric value, could mean higher priority

back to the same node

Connected. Any two nodes are connected by a path

less. 
•
Add an edge, we will have a cycle. Remove an edge, we 
will have more than one tree.

© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026

•
A binary tree is a tree in which every node has at most 
two children. 
•
The two children are distinguished as the
 left child and right child.
•
A binary tree is either:
•
An empty tree, or 
•
A root node together with 
     two disjoint binary trees called its: 
•
left subtree 
•
right subtree

Otherwise, it will be inserted in the right subtree

Deque interface:
•

•

•
void disp_backward(dll_t* ptr_dlist);
•

•
void add_at_end(dll_t* ptr_dlist, int key);
•
int remove_in_begin(dll_t* ptr_dlist);
•
int remove_at_end(dll_t* ptr_dlist);
•
int is_empty(dll_t *ptr_dlist);
•
int is_full(dll_t *ptr_dlist);
•
void deinit(dll_t* ptr_dlist);

•
Deque  interface mapping for stack interface
•
void push(stack_t* ptr_stack, int key);    
=> void add_in_begin(dll_t* ptr_dlist, int key);
•
 int pop(stack_t* ptr_stack); 
=> void add_in_begin(dll_t* ptr_dlist, int key);
•
int is_empty_stack(stack_t* ptr_stack);
=> int is_empty(dll_t *ptr_dlist);
•
int is_full_stack(stack_t* ptr_stack);
=> int is_full(dll_t *ptr_dlist);
•
void init_stack(stack_t* ptr_stack); 
=> void init(dll_t* ptr_dlist);
•
void deinit_stack(stack_t* ptr_stack);
=> void deinit(dll_t* ptr_dlist);

deque
Stack functions delegate to the deque functions
   
© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026 
2 / 9
B.TECH » DEPARTMENT OF COMPUTER SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12
void push(stack_t* ptr_stack, int key)
{
add_in_begin(&ptr_stack->list_, key);
}
int pop(stack_t* ptr_stack)
{
return remove_in_begin(&ptr_stack->list_);
}

Experiential Learning
Round Robin Scheduling
•
Task 3 : Implement scheduling
•
Input : circular doubly linked list of jobs
•
ToDo :
•
Each job shall have time for completion and time allotted so far
•
In the client, support adding a job and processing a job 
•
On processing a job, update the time allotted for the job by the time slice
•
If the time allotted exceeds the time for completion, remove the job 
from the data structure

----------------------------------------------------------------------
[ASSIGNMENT]: 2.3.1_assignment_4.txt
----------------------------------------------------------------------

Assignment : L13-L16 
1. Assignment on doubly linked list 
provide the following functions.
a) add after a key
b) add before a key 
c) remove the key 

2. Assignment on circular list 
a) remove a node with a given if it exists 


3. Assignment on multilist(implementation not part of the syllabus
you may try if you want to challenge yourself) 
input :
name_of_batsman score 
gill 125
jaiswal 55
rahul 50
gill 60
rahul 100
pant 40
...

Observe that the names repeat.
1. create a list of names - each node contains a name and a pointer 
to the node containing the next name 
2. In each node, create a list of scores. The head of the list of 
scores is stored in the node of list of names. Each node of scores 
has a score and a pointer to the next containing the score.

- create such a list 
- display
- find the total for each batsman



----------------------------------------------------------------------
[ASSIGNMENT]: 2.7.1_Week5_assignments.txt
----------------------------------------------------------------------

Week 5 Assignments :
Assignment : L17-L20
queue
priority queue 

------------
1.Reverse a queue so that the front becomes the rear and 
the rear becomes the front. Nodes do not change their position.

2. The component of the queue has an entry time. It could be an integer 
like the epoch time in Unix.
There are two queues which have to be merged based on the entry time.
Provide a function in queue.h and queue.c to support such a merger.

3. There are jobs in the queue to be services which are temporarily 
blocked (may be for IO completion). When their turns come, they will
others to go ahead, but hold the position. When they get unblocked, 
they get serviced.
Can you implement such a variation?

4. implement a priority queue as multilist of queues. There is a list of
queues - one for each priority. Evert new entry will join at the back 
of the queue with the particular priority. Always the first job in the 
highest priority queue shall be serviced.
[degree of difficulty : high; not part of the syllabus]
[ for those who want to challenge themselves]

  
   
   


----------------------------------------------------------------------
[ASSIGNMENT]: 2.13.1_assignment_6.txt
----------------------------------------------------------------------

Week 6 Assignments:
topics: priority queue : experiential learning
a) In our implementations of priority queue, we used two pointers rear and front.
Convert this an implementations with a singly pointer in the list.
One of the operations dequeue or enqueue should be efficient.
Try for both unordered and ordered list implementations.

b) In the priority queue implementation, provide an option to boost the priority 
of existing jobs by 1 because of aging.
The job should provide an interface for boosting.
The queue will traverse and call the function of the job to increase priority for existing
jobs at that point.

c) Complete the round robin scheduling task - which was part of our experiential learning.
